A confirmed prior refresh with a regressed readback fails closed. No replacement token, policy, resource, DNS, Worker, route, or Production traffic is created or changed. - Focused verification after the recovery reconciliation change: web private-database/Admin tests 108/108; Python private-database contract tests 17/17; `git diff --check` passed. No repository-wide TypeScript typecheck was run due the local RAM policy.
- No Cloudflare write, database role update, renewal Job, Staging deployment, or Production traffic mutation has been performed by this code change. Integrated release and authenticated live preflight remain to be verified before any Owner action. After the code is served, run the read-only Staging config recheck. If READY, the one exact next UI action is **Authorize and renew the existing Staging database lease**; inspect the displayed plan and confirm once. The resulting existing canonical Job must be reconciled before **Prepare Staging** becomes eligible.

## Production cutover live blocker recheck — 2026-10-25

- Read-only live probe of `https://runtime.smartaihub.app/readyz` returned HTTP 503 with `activation=disabled`; required bindings `HYPERDRIVE`, `JOB_QUEUE`, `JOB_WORKFLOW`, `JOB_CONTAINERS`, `WORKER_APP`, `MEDIA_BUCKET`, and `VECTOR_INDEX` are absent. This is a real Worker readiness response, not Cloudflare Access denial.
- Linux web/API remain healthy; API `/health` reports the database connected. Production PostgreSQL remains on the existing Linux host/port and was not modified.
- Sanitized `/etc/cloudflared/config.yml` ingress inventory still has only `db-staging.smartaihub.app -> tcp://127.0.0.1:5432`; there is no production DB hostname route. A read-only PostgreSQL TLS handshake on the existing listener presented a leaf certificate for `db-staging.smartaihub.app` (SAN includes staging/localhost/loopback only), issued by `SmartAIHub private PostgreSQL CA`; OpenSSL could not verify the chain (`unable to verify the first certificate`). A production Hyperdrive using a distinct production hostname therefore cannot yet pass strict hostname/CA verification.
- No Cloudflare resource, PostgreSQL setting, Tunnel config, Worker activation, route, or traffic was changed in this check. Do not attach `smartaihub.app/*` while Production `/readyz` is 503; doing so would route real traffic to an unready Worker.
- Next: establish a production-specific DB certificate/hostname and Tunnel ingress on the retained Linux PostgreSQL host, use the existing production Queue (do not provision it again), compose/bind the verified runtime, then prove API/auth/DB and rollback before the existing canary/production route authority is used.

## Artifact publication retry repair — 2026-10-18

- Correction to the prior continuation note: the canonical build pinned to PR #1080 merge SHA `1a31525827a0113827fc0d048b422adf04576fc7` completed `BUILD_PASSED`. Its Cloudflare bundle was `PUBLISH_PENDING`, digest `a0e2a199631ac5664d2b56f6438c8e432b1c926488b656b445752257d941f7a4`, 2 Worker modules and 1,594 public assets. The exact result is `1a31525827a0113827fc0d048b422adf04576fc7-8bc3657d-df04-448b-b5cf-19bfad10b4be.json` in the canonical build results directory. This is a successful build, not artifact publication or live deployment.
- Root cause of publication failure: the packager included the zero-byte `apps/web/dist/public/.gitkeep`; the server-side content-addressed publisher correctly rejected zero-byte manifest entries (`ARTIFACT_MANIFEST_ENTRY_INVALID`). The build receipt then changed to `PUBLISH_PENDING`, while the publisher retry preflight accepted only `BUNDLE_READY`/`PUBLISHED`, making a safe retry impossible.

Other pending reasons remain fail-closed. Publisher output now includes a bounded diagnostic code without exception details or secret values.
- Regression proof on the isolated follow-up branch: Node packager test passes with an empty `.gitkeep` fixture and asserts it is omitted. Focused Vitest now also checks retryable receipt acceptance and unrelated pending-reason rejection. The disposable PostgreSQL executor test remains a DB-backed integration fixture, not full Admin/independent-approval E2E. Rebuild and artifact publication must be repeated from a fresh post-merge canonical SHA; no live Cloudflare deployment or Production change was made.

## Confirmed root cause: account-owned Audit token verified against the wrong API — 2026-10-19 20:28 Asia/Bangkok

- **This supersedes the earlier global missing-Tunnel/DNS-credential blocker.** The Deployment profile verifies as a user token and can deploy the Worker, but exact Tunnel GET returns 401/1001 and DNS GET returns 403/10000 with that credential. The separate existing Audit profile successfully reads BOTH actual Tunnel resources and the root DNS record (HTTP200). These profiles must not be treated as interchangeable credentials or inferred capabilities from one another's results.
- Real root bug: verifyCloudflareToken always called /user/tokens/verify. The saved Audit credential is an account-owned token: that endpoint returns 401, while /accounts/{configuredAccountId}/tokens/verify returns HTTP200/active. The unconditional user verifier therefore rejected a usable saved credential and short-circuited the actual inventory probes. Current official account-token API documents the account-specific endpoint: https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/accounts/subresources/tokens/ . The user's assertion that usable credentials already exist in Admin was correct.
- Repaired verifier supports the exact configured account endpoint after user-verification HTTP401/403 only. It never scans other accounts, accepts an unvalidated account path, or falls back to bypass explicit disabled/expired/future/malformed TTL state or provider outage. Account verification receives the same TTL validation and returns no identifier/secret. The full credential probe now labels Account API token correctly and continues to actual inventory.
- Second root bug: the permission catalog always required a separate user catalog. For a verified account-owned token, that catalog does not apply. The corrected path reads/account-caches only its applicable account catalog, labels the UI accordingly and does not ask the user to add user-scope permissions. User-token behavior still requires the requested account/user results; a denied applicable account catalog remains denied. Cache reuse requires matching applicability and account so it cannot manufacture scope completeness.
